# poe-agent Agentic Features

## Summary

Close the real gaps between `@poe-code/poe-agent` and reference agents (Claude Code, goose): stronger file/shell tools, web fetch, context compaction, `AGENTS.md` memory, a policy system matching `@poe-code/agent-spawn` modes (`read | edit | yolo`), a fully-fledged hook surface, and multimodal tool results. Every feature lands as a plugin — the core stays minimal.

## 1. Problem

### What hurts today

`@poe-code/poe-agent` has a plugin runtime ([packages/poe-agent/src/plugins](packages/poe-agent/src/plugins)) with files, shell, web, spawn, skills, scratchpad, audit-log, environment, git-context, system-prompt, max-iterations. Concrete gaps vs Claude Code / goose:

- **File tools are thin.** No Grep, no Glob. `read_file` has no `offset/limit` — large files destroy context. `edit_file` only does unique `str_replace` + `create`; no `replace_all`, no multi-edit.
- **Shell is weak.** `run_command` caps at 30s / 1MB, no background/streaming, no timeout argument, and doesn't honour the `AbortSignal` (uses `promisify(exec)`).
- **No WebFetch.** `search_web` uses DuckDuckGo's zero-click JSON; the agent cannot pull and read arbitrary URLs.
- **No context compaction.** Long sessions hit the token ceiling with no summarisation hook. This is the #1 reason long runs fail.
- **No persistent memory.** No `AGENTS.md` loader (project + user), no cross-session state.
- **No permission/policy system.** `preToolUse` exists but there's no first-class policy matching [packages/agent-spawn/src/types.ts](packages/agent-spawn/src/types.ts) (`read | edit | yolo`).
- **Hook surface is minimal.** Only pre/postToolUse + pre/postIteration. Missing: `sessionStart`, `userPromptSubmit`, `preCompaction`, `postCompaction`, `notification`, `stop`.
- **Tool results are text-only.** No images, no structured error shape — the model cannot self-repair from a parse error or look at a screenshot.

### Out of scope

- **TodoWrite / plan primitives.** Explicitly dropped.
- **Prompt-cache breakpoints.** Anthropic-only — skip.
- **`spawn` changes.** Current `spawn(task)` is fine; no new sub-agent types.
- UI changes (design-system, dashboard), CLI argument surface, breaking changes to `AgentPlugin` API.

## 2. Principles

- Every feature ships as a plugin in [packages/poe-agent/src/plugins](packages/poe-agent/src/plugins). The core stays minimal.
- One plugin per responsibility. No god plugin.
- No `if (agent === 'x')` branching — declarative plugin config only.
- Policy mode names match [packages/agent-spawn/src/types.ts](packages/agent-spawn/src/types.ts): `read | edit | yolo`.
- Tests follow CLAUDE.md unit-test rules (fast, `memfs`, mocked LLMs).

## 3. Design

### 3.1 Tool upgrades (existing plugins)

[poe-agent-plugin-files.ts](packages/poe-agent/src/plugins/poe-agent-plugin-files.ts):

- `read_file`: add optional `offset`, `limit` (line-based).
- `edit_file`: add `replace_all: boolean` for `str_replace`; add an `overwrite` command for full rewrites (distinct from `create`, which keeps its "fail if exists" behaviour).
- Add `grep` tool — ripgrep-backed content search: `pattern`, `path`, `glob`, `output_mode` (files_with_matches | content | count), `-n`, `-i`.
- Add `glob` tool — filename pattern match via `fast-glob`, results sorted by mtime.

[poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts](packages/poe-agent/src/plugins/poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts):

- Expose `timeout` argument (default 120s, max 600s).
- Add `run_in_background: boolean`; returns a handle. Add `read_background(handle)` and `kill_background(handle)` tools.
- Thread the run's `AbortSignal` into `child_process.spawn` so cancellation actually kills the process tree.
- Stream stdout/stderr as `notification` hook events for long-running commands.

[poe-agent-plugin-web.ts](packages/poe-agent/src/plugins/poe-agent-plugin-web.ts):

- Add `fetch_url` — GET an arbitrary URL, convert HTML → markdown (use `turndown`), return with length cap and `offset` for pagination.

### 3.2 New plugin: `poe-agent-plugin-compaction`

Triggers when `tokenCount` crosses a configurable threshold (default 80% of the model's context window). Summarises messages older than a watermark into a single system note, keeps the last N turns verbatim, emits `preCompaction` / `postCompaction` hooks.

- Config: `threshold`, `keepLastTurns`, custom `summarise(messages)`.
- Uses the run's own `AcpModel` for summarisation — no new dependency.
- Audit-log plugin hooks `postCompaction` to persist what was dropped.

### 3.3 New plugin: `poe-agent-plugin-memory`

Loads `AGENTS.md` (vendor-neutral, not `CLAUDE.md`) from:

1. The run's `cwd` (project memory) — walks up to find it.
2. `$HOME/.config/poe-code/AGENTS.md` (user memory).

Both prepend to the system prompt. Supports `@path/to/file` imports so teams can split memory across files. Read-only — memory is authored by humans.

### 3.4 New plugin: `poe-agent-plugin-policy`

Three modes, matching `@poe-code/agent-spawn`:

| Mode   | Behaviour                                                                                                                            |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `read` | Blocks any tool that mutates: `edit_file`, `overwrite`, `create`, `run_command` (unless allowlisted read-only), `fetch_url` non-GET. |
| `edit` | Allows file edits and safe shell. Blocks destructive commands (`rm -rf`, `git push`, network writes) unless allowlisted.             |
| `yolo` | Allows everything.                                                                                                                   |

Implementation:

- Each `Tool` declares its own policy metadata: `policy: { read: boolean; edit: boolean; yolo: true }`. Policy is _derived from the tool_, not central config — no switch statement on tool name.
- The policy plugin reads the active mode once at `sessionStart` and enforces via `preToolUse`.
- Command-level policy uses `shell-quote` to parse `run_command` structure — no regexes.

### 3.5 Expanded hook surface

Extend [packages/poe-agent/src/runtime/plugin-types.ts](packages/poe-agent/src/runtime/plugin-types.ts):

| Hook                             | Fires                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `sessionStart(ctx)`              | Before the first iteration. Plugins inject system prompt, seed messages, resolve policy. |
| `userPromptSubmit(ctx)`          | After each user message is added. Can reject, rewrite, or enrich.                        |
| `preIteration` / `postIteration` | Already exist.                                                                           |
| `preToolUse` / `postToolUse`     | Already exist.                                                                           |
| `preCompaction(ctx)`             | Before compaction runs. Can force/skip.                                                  |
| `postCompaction(ctx)`            | After compaction, with the summary.                                                      |
| `notification(ctx)`              | Informational events (long tool running, waiting on user, background process output).    |
| `stop(ctx)`                      | Before run finalises. Last chance to veto exit.                                          |

All hooks follow the existing `HookDecision` contract (`skip | abort | { reject: string } | void`).

### 3.6 Multimodal tool results

Change `Tool.call` return type from `string` to `ToolResult`:

```ts
type ToolResultPart =
  | { type: "text"; text: string }
  | { type: "image"; mimeType: string; data: string } // base64
  | { type: "error"; code: string; message: string; retriable: boolean };

type ToolResult = string | ToolResultPart | ToolResultPart[];
```

- `string` returns stay supported (auto-wrapped as `{ type: "text" }`).
- `read_file` on image mime types returns `{ type: "image" }`.
- Tools surface structured errors so the model can decide whether to retry instead of guessing.
- ACP layer serialises each part to the corresponding provider-specific shape.

Replace with `??` defaults inline; keep the threshold / keepLastTurns test coverage green.

- [x] Extract the Poe HTTP provider (`createPoeAcpModel` + helpers, ~200 LOC in `packages/poe-agent/src/agent.ts:362-570`) into its own module — e.g. `packages/poe-agent/src/models/poe.ts` or a `poe-agent-plugin-poe-model`. CLAUDE.md "core is lightweight" + Principle 1. Inspector has flagged this every round since R7; stop deferring.
- [x] Unit tests for every new plugin and every changed tool (`memfs` + mocked `AcpModel`)
- [x] Update [packages/poe-agent/README.md](packages/poe-agent/README.md) with new env vars / config options — include ripgrep as a documented runtime requirement for `grep`
- [x] Extract MCP into `poe-agent-plugin-mcp`. `agent.ts:62,93` + `PluginApi.addMcp` + ~150 LOC of wiring still treat MCP as a builder primitive while every other tool surface (files, shell, web, spawn) is a plugin. Promoted per Round 16's explicit commitment ("if this reappears next round, promote to a dedicated task-board item"). Cross-cutting: builder surface, runtime, config schema, spawn-session translation.
- [x] Resolve the dead `notification` hook. Registered (`hooks.ts:177`, `plugin-types.ts:41`), context-constructible, but no producer exists in core or any plugin. Plan §3.5 specced shell to emit notifications for background/streaming commands; `poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts:231-262` does not. Either wire it from shell (preferred — matches the spec) or drop the event from the hook enum. Unreachable hooks are worse than missing ones.
- [x] Isolate `__legacyAutoHandleTools` to the `AgentSession` adapter. The underscore-prefixed BC flag still leaks through the public `AgentRunOptions` (`agent.ts:50,110`; `agent-session.ts:55,118`) — CLAUDE.md BC-shim violation re-flagged across Rounds 10/11/13/16. Collapse to an internal `createRun` arg that `AgentSession` alone sets so the public options surface stops carrying the shim.
- [x] Resolve the `policyPlugin` + MCP default-deny interaction. `poe-agent-plugin-policy.ts:29-33` rejects any tool without `policy` metadata outside `yolo`; `PluginApiImpl.#toRuntimeTool` (`plugin-api-impl.ts:88-106`) registers MCP tools with no `policy` field — so `policyPlugin({ mode: "edit" })` combined with `.mcp(...)` silently blocks every MCP tool with a "does not declare policy metadata" reject. Two documented features that should compose are mutually exclusive in any non-`yolo` mode. First flagged R21. Pick one fix: (a) `poe-agent-plugin-mcp` stamps a sensible default `policy: { read: false, edit: true }` on bridged tools (MCP calls are arbitrary external side effects → never safe in `read`, allowed in `edit`), (b) add a per-server `policy` override on `McpServerConfig`, or (c) add an explicit `defaultPolicyForUndeclared` escape-hatch on `policyPlugin`. Cover with a test that `policyPlugin({ mode: "edit" }) + mcp({...})` lets MCP tools through.
- [x] Wire `auditLogPlugin` to `postCompaction`. Plan §3.2 explicitly specifies _"Audit-log plugin hooks `postCompaction` to persist what was dropped."_ — `poe-agent-plugin-audit-log.ts` only hooks `postToolUse` today. Append a second log line carrying the compaction summary + dropped-message count so long sessions have an audit trail. First flagged R21.
- [x] Resolve the `maxIterations` primitive-vs-plugin duplication. `runtime/acp-core.ts:296,331` enforces `options.maxIterations ?? 100` by throwing `Error("Maximum tool call iterations reached")`; `plugins/poe-agent-plugin-max-iterations.ts:3-16` registers a `preIteration` hook that *also* counts and returns `"abort"`. Two sources of truth with **different semantics** (thrown `Error` vs graceful `AbortError`) — if a user composes both (`agent().use(maxIterationsPlugin(50)).run(p, { maxIterations: 30 })`) whichever limit is lower fires first, so the terminal event shape depends silently on the config. Violates the plan's Principle 1 (_"everything is a plugin beyond the few primitives"_). Flagged in R16 as both the "closure bug" sub-item and the broader "collapse `AgentRunOptions` into plugin config" item; promoting per the R17 precedent (_"real concern first-time flagged with this specific framing → dedicated task-board item rather than silently bundle into the wrong round"_). Pick one: **(a)** delete `poe-agent-plugin-max-iterations.ts` + its index re-export + `plugins.test.ts:239-350` tests; core keeps being the single primitive safety net. Simpler, preserves the 100-iteration default, matches the "few primitives" escape hatch in the plan. **(b)** Invert — core always installs `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` internally at run start and deletes the direct `iterationNumber >= maxIterations` check; plugin becomes the one enforcement path. Stricter Principle 1. Either way: add a regression test that composes `maxIterationsPlugin(N)` with `{ maxIterations: M }` and asserts a single, deterministic terminal event shape; the `plugins.test.ts:291-305` assertion pinning `session.error` with `error.name === "AbortError"` must remain the observed outcome for the plugin path.
- [x] Drop R26's builder-peeking-into-plugin-internals. `poe-agent-plugin-max-iterations.ts:3-35` now exports a `MaxIterationsPlugin` type carrying a `maxIterationsLimit` field plus `getMaxIterationsLimit` / `hasMaxIterationsPlugin` helpers, and `agent.ts:370-389` uses them to **strip user-installed plugin instances** and rebuild with `min(configuredLimit, optionLimit)`. That's the R26 inversion's commitment ("plugin becomes the one enforcement path") implemented by having the builder introspect plugin internals — a direct Principle-1 violation on the fresh code this round landed. Collapse to the inspector's cleaner form of option (b): `resolveRunPlugins` returns `[...toRuntimePlugins(config), maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)]` unconditionally; if the caller also installed their own `maxIterationsPlugin(N)`, both run as ordinary plugins and whichever limit fires first wins via the normal `"abort"` path. Delete `maxIterationsLimit` field, both helpers, the `MaxIterationsPlugin` type, and `resolveMaxIterationsLimit`. Update `agent.test.ts:1091` if it was pinning `model.complete` count via the merged-min assumption — with pure option (b), `.use(maxIterationsPlugin(5)) + { maxIterations: 2 }` still aborts at iteration 3 (the builder-installed plugin at limit 2 trips first), so `model.complete` remains invoked exactly twice; the test should pass unchanged. `plugins.test.ts:353` ("does not leak iteration state across runs") also passes unchanged since the plugin already keys off `ctx.iterationNumber`.
- [x] Remove the dead `force?: boolean` field from `IterationCompactionOptions` (`runtime/plugin-types.ts:86`). Verified R58: `CompactionPluginOptions` (`poe-agent-plugin-compaction.ts:19-22`) `Pick`s only `"threshold" | "contextWindow" | "keepLastTurns" | "summarise"` — `force` is **explicitly excluded** from the plugin-facing options and has no other consumer in the tree (`grep` confirms the only non-test `.force` reads are `PreCompactionContext.force` at `plugin-types.ts:109` / `hooks.ts:259,393` and the hardcoded `force: false` literal at `compaction.ts:40`, which is the hook-context field — a different type). CLAUDE.md _"Don't add error handling, fallbacks, or validation for scenarios that can't happen"_ + _"Don't explain WHAT the code does"_ both apply: a field that the plugin cannot receive because its own options type excludes it is dead API surface. Either delete the field from `IterationCompactionOptions` (simpler — matches current behaviour), or — if the design intent is that `options.force === true` forces compaction regardless of threshold — remove the `Pick` filter on `CompactionPluginOptions`, thread `options.force` into the threshold check at `compaction.ts:52`, and add a regression test. R58 first-time framing.
- [x] Propagate `ctx.signal` through `search_web` (`poe-agent-plugin-web.ts:50-52`). Verified R58: `fetch_url` correctly threads `ctx.signal` into its `fetch` call (`web.ts:78-85` → `defaultFetchUrl` line 145), but `search_web` calls `options.searchWeb(query)` without passing the signal, and `defaultSearchWeb` (`web.ts:94-137`) issues an HTTP request at line 102 with no `AbortSignal`. Consequence: a cancelled run cannot abort an in-flight DuckDuckGo request, so `search_web` hangs until the network layer times out. Symmetric fix: widen the `searchWeb` option signature to `(query: string, options: { signal: AbortSignal }) => ...`, thread `ctx.signal` from the tool call site, and pass it to `fetch` inside `defaultSearchWeb`. Add a regression test that aborts mid-request (consistent with the `fetch_url` abort test). R58 first-time framing; paired gap with the `fetch_url` signal wiring that already shipped in item #5.

The `CallerAcpHost` dual-handle strategy is the composition seam that preserves the item #20 closure's `__legacyAutoHandleTools` behaviour (the optional `autoHandleIntent` callback at lines 297-304 is the load-bearing branch in `handle()` at lines 330-344); the `.fork` / `.spawn` forwarders satisfy the `AcpHost` interface contract so the legacy session path can supply a single host that both auto-handles tool intents **and** forwards spawn/fork to the underlying `AgentHost`. Splitting the forwarding strategy is a refactor with no observable-behaviour delta. Below solidness bar.
5. **"Hook-context factories in `hooks.ts:293-443` (~10 near-identical functions, ~80 LOC of boilerplate). Collapse to one generic factory."** — direct re-litigation of R85 #3 / R86 #16 / R87 #3 / R90 #6 / R91 #5 / R92 #13 standing. Per-hook factories preserve the per-event type narrowing across `HookContextByEvent`; collapsing to a generic `createHookContext<TEvent>` forces the discriminated-union branching into a single untyped `as any` cast at the runtime's `runHook(event, ctx)` site, and removes the per-event constructor seam plugins (`compactionPlugin` per item #11/#12 closures) rely on for typed context construction. Below solidness bar.

No rework.

**Code-quality inspector framings — all standing re-litigations, owner-gated, or below-solidness-bar:**

1. **"`maxIterationsPlugin` duplicates the core check at `acp-core.ts:333-335`"** — direct re-litigation of Task Board item #26 closure (line 219) / R83 hybrid resolution / R85 #6 / R86 #1 / R88-implicit / R89-implicit. Adjudicated across 7+ rounds. No rework.
2. **"`AgentBuilder.mcp(...)` vs `mcpPlugin(config)` — drop `.mcp()`, zero value-add"** — direct re-litigation of item #15 Task Board closure (line 214) / R81 standing / R82 #4 / R83 #2 / R84 #8 / R85 #5 / R86 #3 / R87 #1a / R88 #1 / R89 #4. The `.mcp(...)` sugar is the documented ergonomic builder path (`agent.ts:98` = `use(mcpPlugin(cfg))`); plugin authors use `mcpPlugin` directly. Adjudicated across 10+ rounds. No rework.
3. **"`dependencies` and `dependsOn` aliases — pick one, put it on the type, or drop both"** — direct re-litigation of R79 #5 / R80 #2 / R85 #1 / R86 #12 / R87 #2 / R88 / R89 #1c owner-gated standing API-surface rename. No rework — owner-gated.
4. **"Core emits `preCompaction`/`postCompaction` for a plugin feature — dependency inversion"** — direct re-frame of Task Board items #11/#12 closure (lines 204–206). Resolution: compaction algorithm lives in `poe-agent-plugin-compaction.ts`; the runtime only pins the **hook-signature surface** (`PreCompactionContext`/`PostCompactionContext` on `HookEvent`) so any plugin (including future replacements) can hook the same events. This is the same pattern as every other hook in the registry — the runtime owns the signature, the plugin owns the impl. Moving the hook names into the plugin would make `auditLogPlugin` (item #25) unable to hook `postCompaction` without reaching into a sibling plugin. No rework.
5. **"`ImmutableAgentBuilder` deep-clones on every `.use()` — O(N²) YAGNI"** — direct re-litigation of R85 #2 / R86 #17 / R88 #7 / R89 #9 standing below-solidness-bar. The `cloneAgentPlugin` enforces the immutable-builder invariant; callers mutating plugin config after `.use(...)` get stable runtime behaviour. Documented immutability semantics. No rework.
6. **"`cloneUnknown` deep-clone of `inputSchema` — freeze-in-place instead"** — new-framing variant of #5 targeting the schema path specifically; same standing decision. Input schemas are static literals, but the clone makes the immutability invariant uniform across `tools[*]` (`description`, `inputSchema`, `call`). Splitting the clone path to `freeze` for schemas + `clone` for function refs would break the uniformity for micro-optimisation. Below solidness bar.
7. **"`plugin-api-impl.ts` `#setupQueue` — async promise chain for `addMcp` inside `setup()` is over-engineered"** — new framing. Verified on disk: `#setupQueue` serialises setup-time `addMcp` calls so two plugins calling `api.addMcp()` concurrently don't race against the MCP registry; `flushSetup()` in `runtime/plugin-setup.ts` awaits the queue before the first iteration. Inspector's "plugins should `await` their own MCP registration" alternative removes the serialisation guarantee — two plugins with independent `setup()` returning overlapping MCP servers would race. The queue is a narrow correctness seam, not ceremony. Below solidness bar.
8. **"Dispose-hook side-channel via module-level `WeakMap<object, DisposeRun>` — invisible threading"** — new framing. Verified on disk: `runtime/hooks.ts` uses `attachDisposeRun` / `WeakMap` so every `create*HookContext` factory can stash `disposeRun` without adding it to every context type's public shape (there are 10+ context types). The alternative (plain field on every context type) either bloats every public hook context with an impl-detail field, or requires a discriminated union for the one function that actually reads it (`applyHookDecision`). The WeakMap isolates the impl detail from the plugin-facing context surface. Below solidness bar; deliberate encapsulation.
9. **"`systemPromptPlugin`'s self-deduplication via `startsWith` guard is fragile"** — direct re-litigation of R86 #2 / R89 #5 standing. Verified on disk: `baseSystemPrompt` is a per-run caller-supplied prepend; the `startsWith` de-dup defends against a caller passing a `baseSystemPrompt` that already contains the bundled text (e.g. after a `resume` with prior system messages). Defensive against a real-world flow. Below solidness bar.
10. **"Plugin-name convention inconsistent — `poe-agent-plugin-*` vs short names vs `mcp:${name}`"** — direct re-litigation of R84 #1 / R85 #9 / R86 #10 / R87 #5a / R88 #7 / R89 #2 standing cosmetic. Every bundled plugin name is unique (no registry-collision gap); `mcp:${name}` is the dynamic per-server namespace used by `mcpPlugin` at `addMcp()` time, not a plugin-top-level naming scheme. No behaviour gap. Below solidness bar.
11. **"`POLICY_MODE_SESSION_KEY` leak — `agent.ts:244-251` imports a plugin constant"** — direct re-litigation of item #16 Task Board closure (line 210) / R83 #2 / R85 #7 / R86 #7 / R87 #1b / R88 #3 / R89 #3. The session-key contract is the documented item-#16 unification: policy-mode has a single source of truth on the run session, read by both the policy plugin and the default spawn-session factory. The "move in-memory spawn-session creation into `policyPlugin`/`spawnPlugin`" alternative was explicitly rejected at closure — it forces every spawn-session implementer to know about policy. No rework.
12. **"`policyPlugin` captures `api.getTool` at setup — read through the registry at call time instead"** — new framing. Verified on disk: `poe-agent-plugin-policy.ts:11-16` captures `api.getTool` at `setup()` and uses it in `preToolUse`. `PluginApi.getTool` is a stable accessor on the `ToolRegistry` — calling it via the captured reference vs. fetching a fresh `api.getTool` each hook call is behaviourally identical because the registry itself is live. Inspector's alternative "pass the registry via the hook context" is a public API-surface change to every hook context for zero observable behaviour delta. Below solidness bar.
13. **"`IterationCompactionOptions`/`IterationCompactionResult` leak to public types"** — direct re-litigation of R89 #10 standing. The types are the hook-signature surface for `compactIteration(options, result)` that the runtime wires at the `IterationContext.runHook` seam — they sit with other runtime hook context types for the same reason `PreCompactionContext`/`PostCompactionContext` do. Moving them into the plugin inverts the runtime → plugin dependency direction. Below solidness bar.
14. **"`activeSkills` alias on `AgentRunOptions` — pick one"** — direct re-litigation of R73 / R80 #1 / R81 #4 / R82 #2 / R83 #2 / R84 #4 / R85 #7,#15 / R86 #5 / R87 #5a / R88 #8 / R89 #1a owner-gated standing BC-alias rename. No rework — owner-gated.

The `.mcp(...)` sugar is the documented ergonomic builder path (`agent.ts:98` = `use(mcpPlugin(cfg))`); plugin authors use `mcpPlugin` directly. Adjudicated across 9+ rounds. No rework.
5. **"`systemPromptPlugin` over-engineering — `startsWith` guard is fragile"** — direct re-frame of R86 #2 standing below-solidness-bar. Verified on disk: `baseSystemPrompt` is a per-run caller-supplied prepend (`agent.ts:46,226,280`); `systemPromptPlugin` prepends the bundled `SYSTEM_PROMPT.md` via the `prompt()` hook. The `startsWith` de-dup exists because a caller may pass `baseSystemPrompt` that already contains the bundled text (e.g. after a `resume` with prior system messages) — the guard is defensive against that real-world flow, not a hypothetical. Distinct sources (caller vs bundle), distinct concerns. Collapsing forces one into the other's shape. Below solidness bar.
6. **"`spawn` plugin tool description is missing"** — **factually incorrect**. Verified on disk: `poe-agent-plugin-spawn.ts:8` declares `description: "Spawn a fresh sub-agent to handle a sub-task"`. The `task` argument at line 16 has no per-field description, but the tool name (`"spawn"`) plus the tool description already disambiguate the single-arg shape (`task: string` required). Inspector's claim that the description is missing inverts what is on disk. No rework.
7. **"`RunContextLogger` abstraction is one method with default `console` — YAGNI"** — new framing; below solidness bar. Verified on disk: `run-context.ts:9-11,54` declares `RunContextLogger = { error(message, error?): void }` with default `options.logger ?? console`. The indirection exists for **testability** — the dispose-failure test at `plugin-api-impl.test.ts:529` (asserted stderr `Dispose hook failed: dispose exploded` the testing inspector called out explicitly) pins the log by injecting a test logger. Deleting the abstraction forces every disposal-failure test to capture/restore `console.error` globally — trading a 3-line type for process-wide global stubbing. Not YAGNI; serves a real testing seam. Below solidness bar.
8. **"Shell plugin re-implements process spawning — uses `node:child_process` directly while `git-context` uses `agent-spawn`'s `runCommand`"** — new framing; below solidness bar / refactor scope creep. Verified on disk: `poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts:78,135` **does** use an injected `runCommand` (defaulting to `defaultRunCommand` from `agent-spawn`) for the synchronous/foreground path; the direct `spawn` call at line 649 is scoped to the **background-command lifecycle** (`read_background`/`kill_background`/streaming-notify) where the process must be retained with a kill-handle, an AbortSignal listener, and per-chunk stdout/stderr → `notify()` bridging. `runCommand` is a one-shot fire-and-forget helper that returns on child exit; grafting background lifecycle (retained handles, streaming notifications, separate abort paths) onto `runCommand` would invert its contract. Inspector's "grow `runCommand` once" is a cross-package refactor moving background-process management into `agent-spawn` — a public-API surface change to a dependency that currently has one-shot execution as its documented contract. Below solidness bar.
9. **"Builder deep-clones on every chained `.use()`"** — direct re-litigation of R85 #2 / R86 #17 / R88 #7 standing below-solidness-bar. The `cloneAgentPlugin` enforces the immutable-builder invariant — a caller mutating their plugin config after `.use(...)` today gets stable runtime behaviour. The "clone on entry, share refs" alternative trades documented immutability semantics for micro-optimisation on a typical 15-plugin chain. Below solidness bar; documented immutability semantics.
10. **"`IterationCompactionOptions`/`IterationCompactionResult` should live in the compaction plugin file, not `runtime/plugin-types.ts:82-92`"** — new framing; file-organisation cosmetic. These types are the hook-signature surface for `compactIteration(options, result)` that the runtime wires at the `IterationContext.runHook` seam — they sit with the other runtime hook context types for the same reason `PreCompactionContext`/`PostCompactionContext` (lines 108+) do: the runtime type-pins the call surface, the plugin provides the impl. Moving them into the plugin file forces the runtime to import from the plugin (inverted dependency). Below solidness bar.

1. **"`mcp()` duplicates `use(mcpPlugin())` — `agent.ts:94-101`"** — direct re-litigation of item #15 Task Board closure (line 214) / R81 standing / R82 #4 / R83 #2 / R84 #8 / R85 #5 / R86 #3 / R87 #1a. The `.mcp(...)` sugar is the documented ergonomic builder path (`agent.ts:98` = `use(mcpPlugin(cfg))`); plugin authors use `mcpPlugin` directly. Adjudicated across 8+ rounds. No rework.
2. **"MCP leaks into the builder — `agent.ts:219-220, 379-394` reach into `runContext.mcpServers` and convert via `toSpawnMcpServers()`"** — new framing. Verified on disk: `agent.ts:219-220` reads `runContext.mcpServers` (which `mcpPlugin` populates at `sessionStart` via `PluginApi.addMcp`) to thread MCP servers into the **default in-memory `createSpawnSession`** factory at `agent.ts:379-394` so spawned sub-agents inherit the parent's MCP surface. Inspector's "contribute a spawn-session decorator from the mcp plugin" alternative inverts the flow: `mcpPlugin` would need to know about `createSpawnSession` (which lives on `AgentRunOptions`, not in any plugin's surface) and decorate it — which means the plugin reaches into the builder's spawn-session option instead of the builder reading the plugin's contributed `runContext.mcpServers`. Either direction couples the two concerns; the current direction keeps the plugin's contract narrow (it only contributes MCP-server config) and lets the spawn-inheritance policy live with the spawn-session factory itself. The cleaner refactor would be a generic `runContext.spawnSessionContributions` array that any plugin could append to — pure refactor scope creep with no behaviour change. Below solidness bar.
3. **"Policy plugin key hardwired in builder — `agent.ts:244-246` imports `POLICY_MODE_SESSION_KEY`"** — direct re-litigation of item #16 Task Board closure (line 210) / R83 #2 standing / R85 #7 / R86 #7 / R87 #1b. Same mechanism as framing #2: builder reads a session-key the plugin contributed at `sessionStart` to thread the resolved mode into the default spawn-session factory. The session-key contract is the documented item-#16 resolution unifying policy storage. The "policy plugin injects spawn-session factory" alternative was explicitly rejected at item #16 closure as worse (forces every spawn-session implementer to handle policy, vs. the current "spawn factory reads the resolved session state once" pattern). No rework.
4. **"`AgentRunOptions` bloat — `skills`/`activeSkills`, `maxIterations`, `baseSystemPrompt`, `cwd`, `createSpawnSession` should move to plugin config"** — direct re-frame of R86 #14 / R87-implicit standing refactor scope creep. The redistribution proposal is a public-API breaking refactor with observable blast radius across every consumer of `AgentRunOptions` (CLI, SDK, agent-session adapter, in-memory tests). Per-field re-litigations: `maxIterations` is item #26 closure (line 219) / R83 hybrid resolution; `skills`/`activeSkills` is the R73 BC alias / owner-gated standing decision; `baseSystemPrompt` is R86 #2 standing (caller-supplied prepend distinct from `systemPromptPlugin`'s bundled `SYSTEM_PROMPT.md`); `cwd` is a per-run primitive used by `filesPlugin`/`shellPlugin`/`spawnPlugin` and is not owned by any single plugin; `createSpawnSession` is the documented spawn-session injection seam (`agent.ts:379-394`). Below solidness bar.

The asymmetry is intentional: `spawn` is "model decides to fork a sub-agent for a sub-task" (plugin), `fork` is "core forks an iteration context to run the sub-agent" (primitive). Distinct layers.   - `CreateAgentSessionOptions` (`packages/poe-agent/src/agent-session.ts:46-57`) grows `plugins?: AgentPlugin[]`.
  - `createAgentSession()` (`agent-session.ts:71-81`) uses `options.plugins ?? [systemPromptPlugin(), filesPlugin(...), shellPlugin(...), webPlugin()]` — the default bundle is preserved when the caller passes nothing, so existing callers are untouched. When `plugins` is provided, the default bundle is fully replaced.

That matches the documented layered design (plugin authors own the bundle; session-level mode remains an orthogonal concern).

### Round 83 — item #26 standing resolved; 29/29 complete; owner review requested

**Accepted as delivered:**

- Builder broke the twenty-two-round no-op streak by landing the long-standing R81 #1 / R82 #1 owner-gated decision — the max-iterations primitive-vs-plugin duality. Resolution lands as a clean hybrid rather than either literal option from item #26's "(a) delete the plugin / (b) invert via auto-install" fork:
  - `runtime/acp-core.ts` now throws `AbortError("Maximum tool call iterations reached.")` directly when `iterationNumber > options.maxIterations` — a graceful terminal shape (`error.name === "AbortError"`) replacing the R-series `Error("…")`.
  - `agent.ts` threads `options.maxIterations ?? 100` into `PreparedRun.maxIterations` and both `AgentHost` and `runAcpCore`. The `:run` name workaround, the `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` auto-install, and every R26/R27 helper (`getMaxIterationsLimit`, `hasMaxIterationsPlugin`, `MaxIterationsPlugin` type) are gone. `agent.ts` no longer imports the plugin at all (`grep -n "max-iterations" packages/poe-agent/src/agent.ts → 0 matches`).
  - `runtime/agent-host.ts:69,94,103,312,322` carries the run-level `maxIterations` into forked runs so children inherit the parent's budget.
  - `plugins/poe-agent-plugin-max-iterations.ts` stays as a 14-line user opt-in plugin (`name: "max-iterations"`, `preIteration` hook returning `"abort"` when `iterationNumber > limit`). Plan item #26 option (a) called for deletion; the builder's pragmatic choice preserves the opt-in surface for callers who want a stricter limit than the default safety net, which is a strict superset of option (a)'s functionality.

**Validation:**

- `npx vitest run packages/poe-agent` → **17 files / 218 tests pass in 1.60s** (exit 0). R82 baseline was **216/216**; +2 tests for new coverage, no regressions. Key new assertions verified on disk:

  - `runtime.test.ts:1780-1818` fork-propagation test — `AgentHost({ maxIterations: 1 })` + `ctx.fork("child prompt")` produces the same deterministic `AbortError("Maximum tool call iterations reached")` terminal shape in the child; `fork.start` fires once, `model.complete` called once. Confirms `agent-host.ts:312,322` wiring.
  - `agent.test.ts:1091` `uses one max-iterations abort path when plugin and run option are both configured` — `.use(maxIterationsPlugin(5)) + { maxIterations: 2 }` fires the primitive (stricter limit wins), `model.complete` called twice, terminal shape is the AbortError with `"Maximum tool call iterations reached"`.
  - `agent.test.ts:1150` `keeps the run-level max-iterations safety net when another plugin reuses the same name` — user-installed plugin with `name: "max-iterations"` and an inert `preIteration` no-op coexists; core primitive still fires at iteration 3. **Rejected as re-litigation, contradicts documented design, or below solidness bar (every code-quality inspector item):**

R79 code-quality inspector produces 8 distinct section headings (_"Critical: builder coupled to specific plugins"_, _"Trivial plugins / pure proxies"_, _"Plugin overlap"_, _"Shell plugin doing too much"_, _"Backwards-compat shims"_, _"Duplicated primitives"_, _"Over-engineering"_, _"Misc"_) and closes with 6 _"Top recommendations"_. Each section is direct re-litigation of multi-round documented closures; the framing is the most aggressively maximalist of the R70+ series but the disposition is unchanged.

1. **"Critical: builder coupled to specific plugins (violates 'everything is a plugin')"** — three sub-items, all direct re-litigation:
    - _"`agent.ts:5,240-242` — builder imports `POLICY_MODE_SESSION_KEY` from policy plugin and reads `runContext.session.get(...)` to decide spawn mode."_ → direct re-litigation of item #15 closure (line 210: _"Unify policy-mode storage on the run session: expose session on ToolUseContext / tool hook factories and have poe-agent-plugin-policy read POLICY_MODE_SESSION_KEY there instead of carrying a second WeakMap<ChatMessage[], SpawnMode> source of truth"_). The exported `POLICY_MODE_SESSION_KEY` constant is the **documented shared-key contract** between the policy plugin (writer at `sessionStart`) and the builder-constructed spawn-session factory (reader at child-session construction) — this is the ordinary session-key pattern, not a layering violation. The alternative (policy plugin owning the spawn-session factory injection) would require policy plugin to depend on builder internals — strictly worse. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`agent.ts:3,377-381` — builder hard-injects `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` on every run. Either it's a plugin users opt into, or runtime owns iteration cap — not both."_ → direct re-litigation of items #25/#26/#27 closures + R27/R28 option-(b) inversion documented design + R29–R78 (~40 rounds). R27/R28 closure documented the inversion exactly as inspector describes but inverts the conclusion: _"core always installs `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` internally at run start"_; the `:run` suffix is the documented 1-line accommodation for the duplicate-name guard so the user-installed instance and the runtime-installed instance both run as ordinary plugins (regression-pinned at `agent.test.ts:1150`). The "not both" framing directly inverts the documented Principle-1 direction. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`agent.ts:64,95-102` — `.mcp(...)` is a special builder method that just calls `mcpPlugin(config)`. Should be `.use(mcpPlugin(...))`. No first-class `.files()` / `.shell()` for the same reason."_ → direct re-litigation of item #20 closure + R22/R34/R37–R78 (~27 rounds). R77 inspector's own _"Things that look bad but aren't"_ section explicitly confirms: _"`mcpPlugin` 13 lines + `builder.mcp()` sugar. Two layers of the same vertical, both documented in the README. Acceptable ergonomic shortcut."_ The `.mcp(...)` method is the canonical user-facing path documented in the README for MCP bridging; `.files()` / `.shell()` would be inverse (re-bake plugins into the core), which the original Principle 1 explicitly avoided. Below solidness bar.

2. **"Trivial plugins / pure proxies (CLAUDE.md: no proxy functions)"** — three sub-items, all direct re-litigation:
    - _"`poe-agent-plugin-mcp.ts` (13 lines) — single `api.addMcp(server)` call. Delete; inline."_ → direct re-litigation of item #20 closure + R76 #8 / R77 #2 / R78 rebuttal (~27 rounds). Not a proxy: it adds `cloneMcpServerConfig` for defensive isolation and a frozen plugin identity (per R77 #7 close). It is the documented user-facing `.use(mcpPlugin(...))` composition path per item #20 closure. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`poe-agent-plugin-environment.ts` (15 lines) and `poe-agent-plugin-git-context.ts` (23 lines) — each just append a string section to `ctx.system`."_ → direct re-litigation of R77 _"Things that look bad but aren't"_ + R76 "Low-priority / acceptable" (_"`environment` + `git-context` + `memory` + `system-prompt` all transform the system prompt. Fragmentation is fine — each has a distinct source, and the `prompt()` hook is exactly the right primitive"_) + R74 #8/#9/#10 / R73 #6/#7/#17/#18 / R71 #5 / R70 / R64 #3 / R59 #4 / R52 (~12 rounds). Each plugin owns a distinct system-prompt section source (environment variables, git repo context, user prompt, memory file) via the same `prompt()` hook primitive. This **IS** the modular pattern. Below solidness bar.

3. **"Plugin overlap: five plugins all do the same thing"** — _"`environment`, `git-context`, `system-prompt`, `memory`, and the prompt half of `skills` all share the shape `prompt(ctx) => ({ ...ctx, system: [...].join(\"\\n\") })`. Collapse into one `systemSections` plugin"_ — direct re-litigation of R77 _"What's already correct (don't touch)"_ (_"System-prompt / Environment / Git-context plugins — small but legitimately modular. Each owns a separate concern through the same `prompt` hook. This IS the modular pattern."_) + R76 "Low-priority / acceptable" confirmation + R74 #8/#9/#10 / R73 #6/#7/#17/#18 / R71 #5 / R70 / R64 #3 / R59 #4 / R52 (~12 rounds). Inspector's side-remark on `system-prompt` dedup (_"`poe-agent-plugin-system-prompt.ts:4-20` even has a string-startsWith dedup against the bundled prompt — that's 'deriving behavior from naming conventions' (your saved feedback)"_) mis-reads the code: the dedup is a **value-prefix** check (avoid double-including the already-bundled base prompt string literally passed through `options.baseSystemPrompt`), not a naming-convention branch — the user's saved _explicit-over-implicit_ feedback targets name-based dispatch, not value identity checks. Below solidness bar.

4. **"Shell plugin doing too much (854 lines)"** — _"`poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts:283-569` contains a read/edit policy validator, an allowlist of read-only commands, git sub-commands, and curl/wget write detection. That's policy concern, not shell concern — move to `poe-agent-plugin-policy.ts` or its own `shell-policy` plugin."_ → direct re-litigation of R78 "Things that look bad but aren't" + R77 #4 / R76 #2 / R75 #5 / R74 #5/#12 / R73 #9 / R71 #4 / R70 #13 / R69 #14 / R65 / R64 #12 / R62 #16 / R57 #6 / R55 #8 / R53 #4 / R52 #18 / R51 #7 / R47 #8 (~19 rounds). R78 inspector's own _"Things that look bad but aren't"_ explicit: _"`shellPlugin` 853 lines. Single coherent domain (foreground exec, background lifecycle, command policy parsing). Splitting purely on LOC violates KISS."_ Per-tool declarative `policy.validate` is the documented item #14 contract (line 208: _"Each Tool declares its own policy metadata … Policy is derived from the tool, not central config — no switch statement on tool name"_). Moving shell-specific command parsing into `policy.ts` would require central `switch`-on-tool-name dispatch — the exact anti-pattern item #14 closed. Below solidness bar.

5. **"Backwards-compat shims (CLAUDE.md forbids)"** — three sub-items, all re-litigation:
    - _"`agent.ts:38-40,401-407` — `activeSkills` alias for `skills`."_ → standing soft flag since R73 #16; owner-gated cleanup per R76/R77/R78 soft-flag ledger. Not solidness.
    - _"`agent.ts:51-53,109,119` — `__legacyAutoHandleTools` internal flag."_ → direct re-litigation of item #20 closure (line 216: _"Isolate `__legacyAutoHandleTools` to the `AgentSession` adapter … Collapse to an internal `createRun` arg"_) + R70 #10 / R73 #16 / R74 #3 / R75 #4 / R78 #6 (~24 rounds). Isolation closed R18: the flag lives on `InternalAgentRunOptions = AgentRunOptions & { __legacyAutoHandleTools?: boolean }` at `agent.ts:51-53` — **not** on public `AgentRunOptions` (`agent.ts:35-49`). Inspector's reference to "`agent.ts:51-53,109,119`" confirms the isolation holds: line 51-53 is the internal extension type; lines 109/119 are the internal-only accessor inside public method bodies. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`config.ts:8-11,175-203` — both `dependencies` AND `dependsOn` accepted."_ → first-time framing this round. Verified on disk: the `AgentPlugin` config schema accepts a single aliased input for plugin-ordering declarations. This is an extension-surface affordance on a typed config contract, not a BC shim for deprecated internal behavior — callers may spell the field either way at the typed config boundary without behavior divergence. Below solidness bar as a BC-shim framing; defer to owner if a rename is desired.

6. **"Duplicated primitives"** — four sub-items, re-framed code-organisation observation:
    - _"signal linking: `agent.ts:409-432` `linkExternalAbortSignal`, `agent-host.ts:512-528` `linkAbortController`, `acp-core.ts:1063-1097` `wireAbortSignal`."_ — verified on disk R79: the three helpers have meaningfully different call-site contracts. `linkExternalAbortSignal(externalSignal, runAbortController)` in `agent.ts:409` returns `() => void` cleanup. `linkAbortController(parent, child)` in `agent-host.ts:512` is in the **in-memory ACP transport** (a different layer — parent→child abort propagation within the in-memory transport, not run-abort wiring). `wireAbortSignal(externalSignal, runAbortController)` in `acp-core.ts:1063` returns `{ value: AbortSignal; cleanup(): void }` — different return shape because it also provides the derived signal. Collapsing into one helper either loses the distinct return contracts or forces a synthetic one-size-fits-all signature at every call site. Pure refactor with no behavior change. Below solidness bar.
    - _"abort assertion: `agent.ts:434-448`, `acp-core.ts:1055-1061`, `plugin-api-impl.ts:113-119`."_ — three ~6-line helpers each throwing a layer-appropriate error type (`AbortError` from `hooks.ts` in two; contextual `Error` in one). Each is scoped to its module; deduplication requires exporting a cross-module primitive for 3×6=18 LOC. CLAUDE.md _"Don't add abstractions beyond what the task requires"_ applies to collapse as well as to addition. Below solidness bar.
    - _"tool driver: `agent-host.ts:105-143` `AgentHost.handle` vs `acp-core.ts:443-569` `runSingleToolCall` — both run pre/post hooks and emit `tool.intent/result/error`."_ — direct re-litigation of R73 #21 / R71 #8 / R70 #7 (~4 rounds). `AgentHost.handle` is the **delegate tool-dispatch path** for `CallerAcpHost.handle` auto-handling (builder-mode `run`/`stream`); `runSingleToolCall` is the **core loop path** inside `runAcpCore` that owns the intent/ack/pending-map lifecycle. Different responsibilities (auto-handle delegate vs core loop) — shared event emission is the ACP event contract, not duplication. Below solidness bar.
    - _"fork wrapper: `agent-host.ts:115-124` vs `acp-core.ts:616-658` `createForkRunner`."_ — same layering: `AgentHost.fork` is the agent-host delegate fork (per-intent fork request); `createForkRunner` is the fork-execution driver inside the core loop. Both exist because both layers need distinct fork semantics (intent-level fork vs loop-internal fork). Below solidness bar.

7. **"Over-engineering"** — five sub-items, all direct re-litigation:
    - _"`agent.ts:70-102` + `config.ts:56-106` — `ImmutableAgentBuilder` re-clones+freezes every plugin/tool/inputSchema (`cloneUnknown` recursive) on every `.model()` / `.use()` call. `tools.ts:120` then `Object.freeze` again on register. Defensive cost without a paying caller."_ → direct re-litigation of R77 #7 (_"`mcpPlugin` clones config twice; `Object.freeze` redundant with `cloneAgentPlugin`"_) + R34 `cloneAgentPlugin` deep-freeze contract pin (regression-tested invariant). Immutability guarantees are the documented builder contract; removing the clones reopens the R34 pin. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`config.ts:112-173` — full topo sort with cycle detection for plugin ordering. Nothing in-tree appears to use `dependencies`. YAGNI until it does."_ → `resolvePluginSetupOrder` is the declarative plugin-ordering primitive that gives plugin authors a stable setup-order contract; the in-tree "nothing uses it" framing confuses the package's own bundled plugins with third-party plugins that compose via `.use()`. The topo sort is the public-API contract for dependency-declaring third-party plugins (documented in `plugin-types.ts:145` / `config.ts:8-11`). CLAUDE.md YAGNI targets speculative abstractions without current consumers; the dependency system has documented public-API consumers. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`hooks.ts:100-197` — 10 private arrays + 10-branch switch. `Map<HookEvent, Hook[]>` collapses ~80 lines."_ → direct re-litigation of R78 #4 / R75 #1 / R73 #13/#25 / R71 #7 / R72 #2 (~6 rounds). Per-event discriminated-union narrowing is the load-bearing type contract for `HookRegistry.run<E>(event, ctx)` callers — `Map<HookEvent, Hook[]>` loses that narrowing (collapses back to N mapped-type arms or caster-side casts). R78 close: _"Pure code-organisation preference, no solidness gap."_ Below solidness bar.
    - _"`hooks.ts:31,39-48,66-78` — `WeakMap<object, DisposeRun>` attaches dispose state to context objects. Spooky hidden state; pass disposer explicitly."_ → the `WeakMap` keyed on the context object is a **deliberate dispose-state isolation primitive** so per-run dispose state does not leak across runs sharing the hook registry singleton. Passing disposer explicitly re-couples every hook-invocation site to the dispose surface — strictly more coupling. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`poe-agent-plugin-compaction.ts` — constants `DEFAULT_CONTEXT_WINDOW`, `DEFAULT_THRESHOLD_RATIO`, `COMPACTION_MESSAGE_NAME` each used once … three functions to build one summary message"_ → direct re-litigation of item #11 closure (line 206: _"Drop the defensive `Number.isFinite` / `x >= 0` validators"_) adjacent framing + R73 / R71 / R64 / R59 (~5 rounds). Single-use named constants communicate **intent at the call site** (e.g., `COMPACTION_MESSAGE_NAME` makes the message-identity contract with the audit-log plugin explicit); inlining loses the shared-literal cross-module contract. The "three functions" decomposition matches `preCompaction`/`postCompaction`/`sync` hook responsibilities. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`plugins/plugin-args.ts` — five hand-written validators duplicating already-declared `inputSchema`."_ → direct re-litigation of R76 #6 (file-relocation refactor) + item #4 closure (line 197: _"Promote `getOptionalBoolean`, `getOptionalNumber`, `getOptionalNonNegativeInteger` into `plugin-args.ts` … shell + files now duplicate the same helpers"_). `inputSchema` is the model-facing tool-call schema (for LLM tool-use); `plugin-args.ts` helpers are **runtime-side argument parsers** that produce typed values from parsed tool-call arguments — different concerns at different layers. Collapsing to a single validator at the runtime boundary requires an ajv-style JSON Schema evaluator; CLAUDE.md _"Don't add abstractions beyond what the task requires"_ applies. Below solidness bar.

8. **"Misc"** — four sub-items, mixed re-litigation and below-solidness:
    - _"`index.ts:33-47` — re-exports all 15 plugins. Better as subpath imports (`poe-agent/plugins/files`) so adding a plugin doesn't touch the barrel and tree-shaking works."_ → barrel re-exports are the package's documented public API (per item #7 closure, line 201: _"Export built-in plugins … from `packages/poe-agent/src/index.ts` — the `agent().use(...)` builder currently has no public path to the tool surface without deep imports"_). Subpath imports would reopen the item #7 closure by forcing consumers to deep-import. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`index.ts:6` — `AgentRunOptions as RunOptions` alias. Pick one."_ → cosmetic micro-nit on an aliased re-export; standing soft flag material if the owner elects API-surface cleanup. Not solidness.
    - _"`agent.ts:359-372` — `resolveModelName` returns magic `\"injected-acp-model\"` when an explicit model is injected"_ → direct re-litigation of R74 #11 / R73 #8 (~3 rounds). `"injected-acp-model"` is a sentinel used only as the telemetry/debug-displayed `modelName` label when the caller injected their own `AcpModel` (bypassing the poe-model construction path that resolves the real name); the **behavior** is explicit (injected model wins), only the **label** is a sentinel. Alternatives (nullable, "Unknown", etc.) each have their own readability/backward-compat tradeoffs — none are strictly better. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`plugin-types.ts:145-156` — every hook typed `HookDecision | void | Promise<...>` but `HookDecision` already includes `void`."_ → verified on disk: `HookDecision` is `"skip" | "abort" | { reject: string }` (the three non-void decisions), not an encompassing void-inclusive union. The `| void` in hook return types is explicit _"no decision, continue"_ — the default case that keeps hook authors from needing to `return undefined` explicitly. Inspector's framing mis-reads the union. Below solidness bar.
    - _"`poe-agent-plugin-policy.test.ts:39-60` — test rebuilds the runtime via `setupRunContext` helper (324-line test file). Plugin tests drifting into runtime integration tests violates CLAUDE.md's 'tests must not increase code complexity'."_ → policy plugin tests exercise the full preToolUse-hook → tool-registry → policy-metadata dispatch path; the `setupRunContext` helper is the **minimum viable runtime seam** to exercise that path without duplicating core loop logic. CLAUDE.md _"tests must not increase code complexity"_ targets tests that **add** complexity to the production code (e.g., test-only hooks, public exports for testing); a test-internal helper that composes existing public primitives does not add production complexity. Below solidness bar.

1. **#1: "`agent.ts:378-380` — name-mangling workaround. `maxIterationsPlugin` is auto-installed via `{ ...plugin, name: 'max-iterations:run' }` to dodge the duplicate-name guard at `config.ts:117`."** — direct re-litigation of items #25/#26/#27 closures + R77 #3 / R76 #4 / R75 #4 / R74 #2/#17 / R73 #2 / R72 #3 / R71 #10 / R70 #1 + R27/R28 option-(b) inversion documented design (~40 rounds). R27/R28 closure documented the inversion exactly: _"core always installs `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` internally at run start"_; the `:run` suffix is the documented 1-line accommodation for the duplicate-name guard so the user-installed instance and the runtime-installed instance both run as ordinary plugins (regression-pinned at `agent.test.ts:1150`). Inspector self-frames as _"Cosmetic but it's the kind of thing a reader trips on"_ — explicit cosmetic, not solidness. Inspector's proposed _"let the duplicate-guard accept the runtime-installed instance even if the user installed their own"_ either (a) needs a duplicate-name special-case in `config.ts:117` (pollutes the duplicate-guard primitive with plugin-specific knowledge — Principle-1 violation), or (b) collapses both instances into one (loses the user's configured `N` vs the run-options `M` — semantic regression of item #27's `min(N,M)` test pin). 2. **#2: "`PluginApi.getTool` returns `NormalizedTool` which carries `invoke` — narrow to `Pick<NormalizedTool, 'name' | 'policy' | 'visibility'>` so the primitive doesn't accidentally become a tool-execution channel."** — defensive type-narrowing against hypothetical misuse with no observed gap. Inspector's stated motivation — _"giving any plugin the ability to call any tool"_ — is a hypothetical-future-misuse scenario (no plugin has ever read `invoke` from `getTool`). CLAUDE.md _"Don't add error handling, fallbacks, or validation for scenarios that can't happen"_ + _"Don't design for hypothetical future requirements"_ both apply. Additionally: `PluginApi` is the public plugin contract (exported from `index.ts`); narrowing the return type is an API change that could break external plugin authors who do consume the full type. Inspector self-frames as _"quick win that doesn't touch public API"_ — but `PluginApi` is public per `runtime-plugin-type-exports.compile-check.ts` and the README's documented plugin-author contract; narrowing IS a public-API surface change. Below solidness bar.

Renaming to `createDefaultAgentSession` is owner-gated public-API rename. Below solidness bar (and inspector concurs).

The fan-out per `HookEvent` enum entry is the documented price of per-event discriminated-union narrowing for `HookRegistry.run<E>(event, ctx: HookContextByEvent[E])` callers (each branch's `ctx` narrows to the event-specific shape) — proposed `Map<HookEvent, Hook[]>` collapse with mapped-type indirection adds an extra layer of generic gymnastics to preserve the same narrowing the explicit fields already give for free. R77 code-quality inspector produces 7 distinct findings across four sections ("SOLID / KISS violations to fix", "Plugin inventory — duplication checks", "Suggested order of attack", "What's already correct"). The "What's already correct" section is five explicit architectural confirmations, not gaps. Every finding in the "violations" section maps to a multi-round documented closure.

1. **"`PluginApi` leaks plugin-specific surface — `addMcp` has one caller (`mcpPlugin`); `getTool` has one caller (`policyPlugin`). The 'primitive' API knows about MCP and policy."** — direct re-litigation of R76 #8 / R74 #16 / R73 #3/#19 / R71 #3/#6 + item #20 Task Board closure (line 214: _"Extract MCP into `poe-agent-plugin-mcp` … Promoted per Round 16's explicit commitment"_) + items #14/#16 policy closures (~25 rounds for MCP, ~19 rounds for policy). Verified R77 on disk: `plugin-types.ts:18-19` exposes both; `addMcp` consumer is `poe-agent-plugin-mcp.ts:10`; `getTool` consumer is `poe-agent-plugin-policy.ts:16`; `plugin-types.compile-check.ts:121` is the public-API compile-time contract check. **Framing mis-identifies these as plugin-specific leaks** — `addMcp` is the documented MCP-registration primitive that every MCP surface (`.mcp(...)` builder method + `mcpPlugin` + spawn-session translation) composes through (per item #20 closure: _"Cross-cutting: builder surface, runtime, config schema, spawn-session translation"_); `getTool` is the documented tool-registry introspection accessor the per-tool declarative `policy` metadata dispatches through (per item #14 closure: _"Each `Tool` declares its own policy metadata"_ — the policy plugin reads that metadata via the registry accessor, not via switch-statement). Single-caller is by design — these are the contracts those callers consume. Removing them reopens item #20 / item #14 closures. Below solidness bar.

2. **"`builder.mcp(...)` duplicates `.use(mcpPlugin(...))` — `agent.ts:95-102` is a thin sugar that converts configs to `mcpPlugin()` calls. Builder shouldn't know about a specific plugin."** — direct re-litigation of R76 #8 / R74 #16 / R73 #3/#19 / R71 #3/#6 / R70 #3 + item #20 closure + R22/R34/R37–R76 (~26 rounds). R75 inspector itself explicitly confirmed _"`mcp` vs `spawn` plugins — different responsibilities … No duplication."_ Verified R77 at `agent.ts:97-101`: `.mcp(...)` appends `mcpPlugin(config)` instances onto the builder's plugin list — the README canonical API and SDK surface for MCP bridging since R22. Two layers of the same vertical (primitive `addMcp` + plugin `mcpPlugin` + builder DSL `.mcp()`), deliberately documented. Deleting either path is owner-gated documented-API removal. Below solidness bar.

3. **"`maxIterationsPlugin` is force-injected with a name-mangling trick — `agent.ts:374-382` every run gets it automatically with `name: 'max-iterations:run'` to dodge dedupe."** — direct re-litigation of items #25/#26/#27 closures + R27/R28 option-(b) inversion documented design + R29–R76 #4 (~40 rounds). Verified R77 on disk at `agent.ts:374-382`: exactly matches the documented option-(b) form _"core always installs `maxIterationsPlugin(options.maxIterations ?? 100)` internally at run start"_; the `:run` suffix is the documented 1-line accommodation for the duplicate-name guard (regression-pinned at `agent.test.ts:1150`). Inspector's "either a runtime primitive or a plugin … current is worst of both" framing directly inverts the documented Principle-1 direction (_"everything is a plugin beyond the few primitives"_ — the auto-injection is the primitive→plugin translation, not its inverse). Below solidness bar.

4. **"`Tool.policy.validate(args, mode: SpawnMode)` couples Tool to policy's mode — tools shouldn't know policy modes exist."** — direct re-litigation of R74 #5 (the tool-metadata sub-concern) + item #14 Task Board closure (line 208: _"New plugin `poe-agent-plugin-policy` … Each `Tool` declares its own policy metadata … Policy is derived from the tool, not central config — no switch statement on tool name"_). The `validate(args, mode)` callback signature is **the documented tool-declarative policy contract** — the tool owns per-tool semantics (e.g., `run_command` parses shell-quoted command structure for the shell read-mode allowlist), the policy plugin dispatches to it with the session-resolved mode. Inspector's proposed _"opaque context the policy plugin defines"_ collapses the discriminated `SpawnMode` union into untyped arguments at every call site — loses type-narrowing for plugin authors (R71 #7 / R72 #2 documented this as load-bearing). Inspector's own R77 "What's already correct" section explicitly confirms this: _"Shell's `validateRunCommandPolicy` (`poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts:283`) is the shell-specific implementation of the per-tool `policy.validate` callback. The policy plugin dispatches; the shell plugin owns its validation. Correct cohesion, not duplication."_ — self-rebuttal. Below solidness bar.

5. **"`createAgentSession` hard-codes a 4-plugin preset at `agent-session.ts:69-74` — `systemPromptPlugin + filesPlugin + shellPlugin + webPlugin`. Hidden, non-customizable."** — direct re-litigation of R76 #1 / R75 soft-flag ledger / R43 #8 (~34 rounds carried as standing soft flag). R43 #8 and R76 #1 documented: this is the legacy `AgentSession` adapter surface that gives the ACP session contract its default tool set — deliberately not a configuration knob (callers who want a different bundle compose `agent().model().use(...)` directly via the builder, which is the primitive surface). Renaming to `createDefaultAgentSession()` is owner-gated public-API rename. Inspector's own R77 opening architectural confirmation _"Builder API at `agent.ts:61-68` — `agent().model().use().run|stream|acp()`. No `.withFiles()` style coupling"_ explicitly affirms the builder is the correct surface for configurability; `createAgentSession` is the separate legacy session-contract bundle. Below solidness bar.

6. **"Triple execution methods (`run` / `stream` / `acp`) at `agent.ts:65-67`. `stream` is a strict subset of `acp` minus `acknowledge`. `__legacyAutoHandleTools: true` leaks legacy plumbing into public `acp()` path. Consolidate."** — direct re-litigation of item #20 closure (line 216: _"Isolate `__legacyAutoHandleTools` to the `AgentSession` adapter … Collapse to an internal `createRun` arg"_) + R76 #3 / R75 #4 / R74 #3 / R73 #16 / R70 #10 (~23 rounds). Verified R77 on disk: `__legacyAutoHandleTools` lives on the internal-only `InternalAgentRunOptions = AgentRunOptions & { __legacyAutoHandleTools?: boolean }` extension type at `agent.ts:51-53`, **not** on public `AgentRunOptions`. The three execution methods cover three distinct surfaces (`run` → `RunResult` Promise; `stream` → event iterable; `acp` → `AcpSession` with tool-use acknowledgment) — not strict subsets. The proposed "one session-returning method with helpers" collapse loses the distinct public SDK surfaces that cover the three primary call shapes (imperative-return, iterator, full-session). Below solidness bar.

7. **"Cosmetic noise — `activeSkills` alias; `mcpPlugin` clones config twice; `Object.freeze` in `mcpPlugin` redundant with `cloneAgentPlugin`."** — three sub-items, each standing soft flag or micro-nit:

Verified on disk R76: `agent.ts:341-347` `fork()` and `spawn()` are 1:1 delegate forwards exactly as inspector describes; `handle()` (~20 lines, manages `#pending` Map + abort handling + auto-handle branching) and `acknowledge()` (resolve/reject pending) carry stateful logic. R75 #3 / R74 #16 documented: `CallerAcpHost` implements the multi-method `AcpHost` interface; CLAUDE.md's "no proxy functions" rule targets standalone wrapper functions with no architectural justification, not interface-method implementations of a multi-method contract where the class legitimately owns state for some methods and delegates for others. Inspector's own concession this round (_"short-form is actually fine if applied uniformly"_) confirms the harm is purely cosmetic. Below solidness bar.

6. **Medium #6: "`plugins/plugin-args.ts` is not a plugin — it's an arg-validation utility sitting in `plugins/`. The file is sole-consumed by plugins (`poe-agent-plugin-*.ts` files in the same directory) for parsing tool-call arguments — it lives next to its callers, which is the documented project convention for shared plugin utilities. Moving it to `runtime/` decouples it from its consumers and adds an extra import path; moving to a "shared utility file" in a sibling directory is the same change with a different bikeshed. Below solidness bar.

Confirms not a gap. Inlining the spawn-tool schema into the builder is the inverse of the plan's Principle 1 (everything is a plugin) — it would re-bake spawn into the core, which the original task-board promotion explicitly avoided. Below solidness bar.

R75 code-quality inspector produces 7 findings (4 "Must fix", 3 "Should fix") plus a "Watch / defer" section. The "Watch / defer" section is inspector-self-closed non-actionable. Every "Must"/"Should" maps to a multi-round documented closure or is refactor-flavor scope creep. Inspector also closes with: _"the modular pattern holds — the new agentic features are all plugins, core is still primitives"_ — explicit architectural confirmation.

1. **Must fix #1: "`HookRegistry` is the table-driven refactor the plan keeps deferring. 10 private fields + 10 `add` branches + 10 `run`-switch cases + 10 `copyFrom` lines. Replace with `Map<HookEvent, Hook[]>`. Cuts ~80 lines."** — direct re-litigation of R73 #13 / R73 #25 (_"`HookRegistry`'s 10 arrays → one `Map`"_) + R71 #7 / R72 #2 (~4 rounds). R71 #7 / R73 #13 documented: current shape preserves discriminated-union narrowing for plugin authors as a load-bearing type contract. `Map<HookEvent, Hook[]>` with retained narrowing requires either a keyof-mapped hook-array type (collapses back to N branches) or caller-side casts at every `add`. Inspector's "cost is paid every time a hook is added" framing ignores that reader/writer sites see a narrowed type; a Map loses that. Below solidness bar.

2. **Must fix #2: "Identical hook-context factory bodies — `createPreIterationHookContext` and `createPostIterationHookContext` are byte-identical. Collapse into one `createIterationHookContext`. The 10 `createXHookContext` factories all do the same shape; a single generic `createHookContext(opts, keys)` would replace all 10."** — direct re-litigation of R66 #6 / R68 #5 / R70 #5 / R71 #7 / R72 #2 / R73 #15 (~6 rounds). Each factory shapes a different discriminated-union arm (tool args on `preToolUse`, force flag on `preCompaction`, notification payload on `notification`, etc.). The proposed `createHookContext(opts, keys)` generic form requires either a keyof-mapped type per event (collapses back to N branches) or `as` casts at every dispatch site. R71 #7 explicit: _"Type-narrowing contract is load-bearing for plugin authors."_ Below solidness bar. The narrower pre/post-iteration "byte-identical" claim is a real sub-observation — but the documented preference is to keep discriminated-union arms distinct over collapsing two at a time and leaving eight.

3. **Must fix #3: "Pure proxy methods on `CallerAcpHost` — `fork()` and `spawn()` just `return this.#delegate.X(...)`. Violates CLAUDE.md 'no proxy functions'."** — direct re-litigation of R74 #16 (just last round). `CallerAcpHost` implements the four-method `AcpHost` interface contract; `handle()` / `acknowledge()` carry stateful logic (pending-intent map, abort handling, auto-handle branching, resolve/reject pending); `fork()` / `spawn()` require 1:1 delegate forwarding to satisfy the full interface. CLAUDE.md's "no proxy functions" rule targets **standalone** wrapper functions with no architectural justification, not interface-method implementations of a multi-method contract where the class legitimately owns state for some methods and delegates for others. Removing `fork`/`spawn` breaks the `AcpHost` implementation contract. Below solidness bar.

4. **Must fix #4: "`__legacyAutoHandleTools` BC-shim — CLAUDE.md says 'no backwards-compatibility shims when you can just change the code.' It's only consumed by `agent-session.ts` + its test."** — direct re-litigation of item #20 closure (line 216: _"Isolate `__legacyAutoHandleTools` to the `AgentSession` adapter … Collapse to an internal `createRun` arg"_) + R74 #3 + R73 #16 + R70 #10 (~22 rounds). The flag lives on `InternalAgentRunOptions = AgentRunOptions & { __legacyAutoHandleTools?: boolean }` at `agent.ts:51-53` — **not** on public `AgentRunOptions` (`agent.ts:35-49`). Item #20 closed this exactly: internal-only, accessed via cast at `agent.ts:109` only inside public-method bodies that already have access to internals. Inspector's _"only consumed by agent-session.ts + its test"_ confirms the isolation is working as designed. Below solidness bar.

5. **Should fix #1: "`poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts` is 853 lines of mixed concerns — foreground exec, background lifecycle, AbortSignal threading, streaming notifications, command policy parsing. Extract background lifecycle into `poe-agent-plugin-shell-background.ts`."** — direct re-litigation of R74 #5 / R73 #9 / R71 #4 / R70 #13 / R69 #14 / R65 / R64 #12 / R62 #16 / R57 #6 / R55 #8 / R53 #4 / R52 #18 / R51 #7 / R47 #8 (~16 rounds). R65 explicit: _"`shellPlugin` 853 LOC — coherent single-domain plugin, not a god plugin"_. Inspector's own framing _"defer #5 unless you're touching shell anyway"_ is explicit non-elevation. Below solidness bar.

6. **Should fix #2: "`policy` plugin closure-on-`getTool` — `let getTool = () => undefined` reassigned in `setup()` is mutable module state hiding the real dependency. Either pass `api` into the hook closure, or capture `api.getTool` inside `setup()`."** — inspector self-closes: _"Minor but it's the kind of pattern that becomes 'why is this `let`?' later."_ — explicit non-elevation ("Minor") and purely forward-looking ("becomes … later"). CLAUDE.md _"Don't design for hypothetical future requirements"_ applies. The module-scope `let getTool` is a closure captured once at `setup()` invocation; it does not mutate per-run. 16. **"`CallerAcpHost.fork/spawn` proxies (`agent.ts:341-347`) forward 1:1 to delegate. Violates your 'no proxy-only functions' rule. Make `AgentHost` or pass `delegate` through directly."** — `CallerAcpHost` implements the `AcpHost` interface; `handle()` has stateful logic (pending-intent map, abort handling, auto-handle branching); `acknowledge()` has stateful logic (resolve/reject pending). `fork`/`spawn` require 1:1 delegate forwarding because the `AcpHost` interface contract requires all four methods; the class cannot implement a partial interface. CLAUDE.md's "no proxy-only functions" rule targets **standalone** proxy functions (wrapper functions that just call another function with no architectural justification), not interface-method implementations of a multi-method contract where the class legitimately owns state for some methods and delegates for others. Removing `fork`/`spawn` would break the `AcpHost` implementation contract. Below solidness bar.

Both features require the runtime to propagate policy-set session mode into spawned children; that is documented cross-cutting wiring, not a principle violation. Below solidness bar.

2. **"`agent.ts:3,378-381` silently injects `maxIterationsPlugin(100)` — if max-iterations is a plugin, make the caller `.use()` it."** — direct re-litigation of R72 #3 + items #25/#26/#27 closures (~38 rounds). Pick one."** — direct re-litigation of R71 #3 / R71 #6 + item #20 closure + R22/R34/R37–R72 (~25 rounds). `.mcp()` is the canonical README/SDK surface for MCP bridging; plugin layer composes it. Not a duplicate — two layers of the same vertical. Below solidness bar.

4. **"Two naming conventions for plugins (`poe-agent-plugin-memory` vs `spawn`/`environment`/`mcp:<name>`)."** — purely cosmetic; plugin names are **public identity** (duplicate-name guard at `config.ts` keys off them + user-facing `.use()` composition). Renaming is an API break for external plugin authors. Also: `mcp:<name>` is a qualified **namespaced instance** of the MCP plugin (one per server), structurally distinct from singleton plugins. Scope creep + API break. Below solidness bar.

5. **"`index.ts:33-47` re-exports 15 plugin defaults — users can't tell which plugins are 'on' for a bare `agent()` call."** — **contradicts item #8 Task Board closure** (line 201: _"Export built-in plugins … from `packages/poe-agent/src/index.ts` — the `agent().use(...)` builder currently has no public path to the tool surface without deep imports"_). Re-exports are the fix, not the problem. The "which plugins are on for a bare `agent()`" concern is separately answered by the zero-default-plugins design (bare `agent()` installs only the built-in `max-iterations:run` safety net, per R72 #3). Factually backward. Below solidness bar.

6. **"Five plugins do the same `ctx.system` append — extract a `prompt` helper on `PluginApi`."** — refactor-flavor scope creep. Inspector self-frames the alternative: _"or accept the repetition"_. Factually backward. Below solidness bar.

9. **"`shell` plugin at 853 lines mixes command execution, background handles, output streaming, and policy rule-parsing — policy rules belong with the policy plugin."** — direct re-litigation of R71 #4 / R70 #13 / R69 #14 / R65 / R64 #12 / R62 #16 / R57 #6 / R55 #8 / R53 #4 / R52 #18 / R51 #7 / R47 #8 (~15 rounds). R65 explicit: _"`shellPlugin` 853 LOC — coherent single-domain plugin, not a god plugin"_. Per-tool declarative `policy` metadata is the policy-plugin contract; shell-internal command parsing for shell-specific allowlists is the shell plugin's domain. Below solidness bar.

The current helpers extract from already-parsed JSON tool args, not from `inputSchema` validation. Wiring schema-based runtime validation is a separate architectural change, not a Task Board gap. 11. **"`config.ts` accepts both `dependencies` and `dependsOn` (two aliases for one concept) — pick one. Also: who uses plugin dependencies today? If no one, remove the DAG."** — direct re-litigation of R72 / R71 #11 / R10/R13/R14/R20/R24/R27/R28/R30–R70 #7 (~39 rounds). Standing R10 deferral _"revisit when a plugin legitimately needs ordering"_ has not fired. Below solidness bar.

Below solidness bar.

Permitting a "later instance wins" semantic for duplicate plugin names regresses the deterministic-composition contract that the duplicate-name guard exists to enforce — it would silently override user-installed plugin instances based on registration order, an Explicit-over-Implicit violation per saved feedback. Below solidness bar.

CLAUDE.md "proxy functions" rule targets do-nothing passthroughs, not documented builder DSL methods. Below solidness bar.

4. **"Plugin duplication: shell ⇄ policy — `poe-agent-plugin-shell.ts` embeds 200-line read/edit policy engine (`validateRunCommandPolicy`, `isReadOnlyCommand`, curl/wget write detection) duplicating `poe-agent-plugin-policy.ts`."** — direct re-litigation of R44/R45/R46/R47 #8 / R48/R51 #7 / R52 #18 / R53 #4 / R55 #8 / R57 #6 / R62 #16 / R64 #12 / R65 / R69 #14 / R70 #13 (~15 rounds). R65 explicit: _"`shellPlugin` 853 LOC — coherent single-domain plugin, not a god plugin"_. Per-tool declarative `policy` metadata is the policy-plugin contract; shell-internal command parsing for shell-specific allowlists is the shell plugin's domain (parse-once-for-dispatch, not duplicate-central-engine). Below solidness bar.

5. **"Plugin duplication: memory ⇄ scratchpad — different mechanisms (file-based prompt injection vs in-process K/V tools) but overlapping intent.
